❯ /plan                                                                         
  ⎿  Enabled plan mode

❯ Let's build the tech lead agentic workflow. Prepared for production - with    
  evals and observability and audit. The project should be following the latest 
   findings in the agentic production development in 2026 and mark              
                                                                                
  Set up a docker-compose with Jira and Gitlab containers with some sample data 
   in them. Set up a team of four - John, Matt, Alicia, Karen. Database for     
  daily storage - sqlite. Storage layers are below:                             
                                                                                
  ┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐              
  │  LAYER 1 — Flat markdown files (the "configuration")         │              
  │  ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────   │              
  │  Team facts, ownership, escalation rules, TL preferences     │              
  │  Updated manually or by explicit "remember this" actions     │              
  │  Loaded fresh into context each run                          │              
  └──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘              
                                                                                
  ┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐              
  │  LAYER 2 — SQLite (the "state")                              │              
  │  ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────   │              
  │  Tables:                                                     │              
  │   - daily_flags(date, engineer_id, type, status, days_hot)   │              
  │   - predictions(date, claim, resolved_date, outcome)         │              
  │   - standup_observations(date, engineer_id, raw, summary)    │              
  │   - engineer_baselines(engineer_id, window, metric, value)   │              
  │   - ticket_snapshots(date, ticket_id, status, assignee, …)   │              
  │   - decisions(timestamp, hot_spot, response_mode, outcome)   │              
  │  Use FTS5 for free-text search on standup_observations                      
                                                                                
                                                                                
  ═══ PHASE 0 — Loop closure (do this FIRST) ═══                                
                                                                                
  Load from memory: yesterday's flags, open questions, predictions              
  └─ For each item: resolved? still hot? escalation candidate (>N days)?        
  └─ Carry forward unresolved items into today's working set                    
                                                                                
                                                                                
  ═══ PHASE 1 — Collect raw signals (parallel, deterministic) ═══               
                                                                                
  → Today's standup text, per engineer                                          
  → Yesterday's standup text, per engineer (from memory)                        
  → Sprint state from Jira:                                                     
    - All sprint tickets: status, assignee, last update, history of estimate    
  changes                                                                       
    - Tickets added since yesterday → who added, when                           
    - Ticket dependency graph (blocks / blocked-by)                             
  → Gitlab commits in window (yesterday 12pm → today 12pm):                     
    - Author, timestamp, branch, message, diff size, files touched              
    - Linked ticket (parsed from commit message, if present)                    
  → Sprint timeline: day N of M, planned vs. completed points                   
                                                                                
                                                                                
  ═══ PHASE 2 — Per-engineer rough triage (LLM, parallel —                      
  ──── (136 lines hidden) ───────────────────────────────────────────────────── 
  s, latencies, tool args/results. If I can't reconstruct a bad run from the    
  logs, they can't either.                                                      
    - Idempotency and dedup on tool calls. Especially for anything that writes  
  (Stripe, Salesforce). Did they think about "what if the LLM retries this"?    
                                                                                
    Memory                                                                      
    - Is there a distinction between working context, durable state, and        
  references-to-fetch? Or is it one growing list of messages? Compaction        
  strategy matters once runs go past a few turns.                               
                                                                                
    Integrations (if present)                                                   
    - OAuth that actually refreshes. Webhook handler that's idempotent and      
  signature-verifies. Schema mapping that handles drift, not just a frozen      
  fixture. Error handling that distinguishes 429/5xx/auth/validation.           
                                                                                
    Sandboxing                                                                  
    - If the agent executes code or shell, how is it isolated? Docker, Modal,   
  Firecracker, subprocess with rlimits — any answer is fine, "we just run it"   
  is not.                                                                       
                                                                                
    Their own agentic coding hygiene                                            
    - .claude/, CLAUDE.md, custom slash commands, an MCP server, agent          
  definitions, hook scripts. Evidence they build for themselves, not just       
  consume. This is the single highest-signal directory in the repo.             
    - Commit cadence. Lots of small commits with real messages > one "initial   
  commit" of 8k lines. Speed is observable in git history.                      
                                                                                
    Prompts                                                                     
    - Version-controlled, modular, with at least some notes on why a phrasing   
  was chosen. Inline 200-line f-strings with no provenance is a junior pattern. 
                                                                                
    Judgment signals                                                            
    - What they didn't build. A 4-layer abstraction for a single use case is    
  worse than 200 lines of direct code.                                          
    - README explains the tradeoffs they made, not just the features. "We chose 
   X over Y because Z" beats a feature checklist.                               
    - Range: do they have a frontend, a deploy config, an infra script? Or is   
  it backend-only?                                                              
                                                                                
    Red flags                                                                   
    - LangChain/CrewAI wrapper with no novel runtime work — they've never had   
  to debug the layer that matters.                                              
    - "Works on demo data" — no real API in the loop.                           
    - No evals, no traces, no tests. Means they ship by vibes and can't recover 
   from a regression.                                                           
    - One model hardcoded throughout. I want to see at least two providers      
  swapped in commits, or a model-router abstraction.                            
    - A tools/ directory with 40 tools that all look identical and clearly      
  copy-pasted, with no shared validation/error layer.                           
                                                                                
    The conversation I'd want to have after                                     
    - "Tell me about a time the agent confidently lied to you. How did you      
  catch it, and what did you change?" The repo should make that answerable in   
  30 seconds of file-pointing.

❯ Is it worth to use Mattermost instead of Slack? 

⏺ Short answer: yes, worth it. Real Mattermost (self-hosted docker image + its
  own Postgres) gives you real REST + real webhooks + real OAuth path — directly
   ticking the "OAuth that refreshes / webhook idempotency / signature
  verification / no demo-data, real API in the loop" senior signals. The
  trade-off is one extra heavyish container and slightly more work to reset
  state between eval scenarios (handled via a postgres-volume snapshot restored
  per case, or API-driven reset).

  If you go this way it parallels the GitLab choice nicely (real product, mock
  only the painful one — Jira). Let me confirm before locking the plan.

⏺ User declined to answer questions
  ⎿  · Swap mock-Slack for real Mattermost in compose? (Yes — real Mattermost 
     (Recommended) / Keep mock-Slack (FastAPI) / Real Mattermost AND keep a thin
      recorder shim)

⏺ What would you like to clarify about the Mattermost option? Happy to dig into
  any of these angles:

  - Boot/resource cost of adding Mattermost + its Postgres to compose (vs. what
  we already have: GitLab CE + Phoenix + jira_mock + sqlite + optional Ollama)
  - How eval determinism is preserved when the chat layer is a stateful real
  product (snapshot/restore strategy vs. API reset vs. recording shim)
  - Which "senior signals" Mattermost actually unlocks that mock-Slack would not
   — concretely: OAuth refresh path, webhook signature verification, idempotent
  webhook handler
  - How Phase 8 (TL approval → post DM → readback → audit) changes if we use
  Mattermost instead
  - Alternatives I didn't list (Rocket.Chat, Zulip, real Slack via a workspace
  token, etc.) and how they compare

  Tell me what you want to know more about, or share the consideration that's
  making you hesitate, and I'll address it before locking the plan.

✻ Brewed for 5m 41s

❯ how valuable is it to have support in 2026 for Mattermost instead of Slack as 
   first priority implementation?                                               

⏺ Honest read: moderate value, leaning low as a primary-target choice in 2026.

  Slack still owns roughly 75–80% of tech-org chat mindshare; Mattermost has
  grown meaningfully in regulated/enterprise/EU-sovereignty contexts but remains
   a minority deployment. Mattermost-first signals one strong thing — that you
  wanted a self-contained dev loop with no external workspace, real
  OAuth/webhook surfaces you can hit offline, open-source semantics — which is a
   genuine architectural win for the eval harness and reproducibility story. But
   it only reads as a strong hiring signal if you ALSO ship a thin Slack adapter
   behind the same interface; otherwise a reviewer reasonably wonders whether
  you avoided the harder, real-world target (Slack-specific gotchas:
  signing-secret replay windows, Events API vs Socket Mode, interactive-payload
  format, per-workspace rate limits — you don't earn those scars from
  Mattermost).

  My recommendation for this build: chat-provider-agnostic interface in 
  tools/chat.py, Mattermost as the in-compose default (because it solves the
  eval-determinism + offline-dev problem), and ship a stub Slack adapter that
  demonstrates the seam — README documents that the Slack adapter is "wired but
  not gated by tests because we don't seed a real workspace." That captures both
   signals: the thoughtful self-hosted default AND evidence you understood the
  dominant real-world target. The cost is small — maybe a day's work for the
  Slack adapter stub plus a paragraph in the README.

  Want me to update the plan to that shape (Mattermost in compose as primary,
  ChatProvider abstraction, Slack stub adapter), or stay with the original
  mock-Slack?

     │ │   │   ├── verifier.py          # separate LLM call: re-reads artifact │
     │  + tool trace;                                                          │
     │ │   │   │                        # returns PASS|RETRY_WITH_HINT|FAIL.   │
     │ │   │   │                        # Detects "claimed success without     │
     │ matching tool call".                                                    │
     │ │   │   └── concurrency.py       # asyncio fan-out; semaphore per       │
     │ provider                                                                │
     │ │   │                                                                   │
     │ │   ├── phases/                  # the 8-phase pipeline — orchestrator  │
     │ wires them                                                              │
     │ │   │   ├── orchestrator.py      # runs P0→P8 with per-phase OTel spans │
     │ │   │   ├── phase0_loop_closure.py    # deterministic                   │
     │ │   │   ├── phase1_collect.py         # deterministic; asyncio.gather   │
     │ │   │   ├── phase2_triage.py          # LLM per-engineer in parallel;   │
     │ Haiku 4.5                                                               │
     │ │   │   ├── phase3_correlate.py       # single Sonnet 4.6 call over P2  │
     │ output                                                                  │
     │ │   │   ├── phase4_reconcile.py       # deterministic counter math      │
     │ │   │   ├── phase5_deepdive.py        # ReACT, Opus 4.7, tools,         │
     │ verifier                                                                │
     │ │   │   ├── phase6_response_mode.py   # Sonnet, structured →            │
     │ ResponseMode enum                                                       │
     │ │   │   ├── phase7_compose.py         # Sonnet, drafts brief + DMs +    │
     │ standup pts                                                             │
     │ │   │   └── phase8_execute.py         # await TL approval →             │
     │ deterministic executor + readback                                       │
     │ │   │                                                                   │
     │ │   ├── obs/                     # observability                        │
     │ │   │   ├── tracing.py           # OpenTelemetry setup; OTLP → Phoenix  │
     │ │   │   ├── spans.py             # decorators: @phase_span, @tool_span, │
     │  @llm_span                                                              │
     │ │   │   ├── metrics.py           # tokens, latency, cost,               │
     │ cache_hit_rate per span                                                 │
     │ │   │   └── jsonl_sink.py        # also dumps every span to JSONL —     │
     │ git-diffable                                                            │
     │ │   │                                                                   │
     │ │   └── web/                     # Phase 8 review UI — FastAPI + HTMX   │
     │ │       ├── app.py                                                      │
     │ │       ├── routes/                                                     │
     │ │       │   ├── review.py        # /brief, POST /approve/<id>,          │
     │ /reject/<id>, /edit/<id>                                                │
     │ │       │   ├── decisions.py     # audit log view                       │
     │ │       │   └── replay.py        # /trace/<id> — span tree viewer       │
     │ │       └── templates/           # Jinja2 + HTMX; no SPA build          │
     │ │                                                                       │
     │ ├── services/                    # compose services we own              │
     │ │   ├── jira_mock/                                                      │
     │ │   │   ├── app.py               # FastAPI; Jira REST subset (issues,   │
     │ sprints, comments, history)                                             │
     │ │   │   ├── seed.py              # deterministic fixtures: ~30 tickets  │
     │ across sprint                                                           │
     │ │   │   ├── fixtures/*.json                                             │
     │ │   │   └── README.md            # "speaks: GET /rest/api/3/issue/*,    │
     │ ..."                                                                    │
     │ │   └── mattermost_seed/         # we DON'T author Mattermost —         │
     │ official image in compose                                               │
     │ │       ├── seed.py              # uses mmctl/REST: create team,        │
     │ channels, 4 bot users                                                   │
     │ │       │                        # (john/matt/alicia/karen), 7 days of  │
     │ standups × 4                                                            │
     │ │       ├── webhook_target.py    # tiny FastAPI: receives Mattermost    │
     │ outgoing webhooks                                                       │
     │ │       │                        # for the TL_review channel;           │
     │ signature-verify + idempotent                                           │
     │ │       ├── fixtures/standups.yaml                                      │
     │ │       └── README.md            # "speaks: real Mattermost v4 REST;    │
     │ OAuth refresh tested"                                                   │
     │ │                                                                       │
     │ ├── infra/                                                              │
     │ │   ├── docker-compose.yml       # gitlab-ce, jira_mock, mattermost,    │
     │ mattermost-postgres,                                                    │
     │ │   │                            # phoenix, tl_agent_web, ollama        │
     │ (profile)                                                               │
     │ │   ├── gitlab/                                                         │
     │ │   │   ├── seed.sh              # creates project, pushes commits via  │
     │ git; assigns to engineers                                               │
     │ │   │   └── commits.yaml         # scripted commit timeline matching    │
     │ the eval scenarios                                                      │
     │ │   ├── mattermost/                                                     │
     │ │   │   ├── config.json          # Mattermost server config (enable     │
     │ OAuth, webhooks, bot accounts)                                          │
     │ │   │   └── snapshot.sh          # pg_dump after seed → per-eval-case   │
     │ restore for determinism                                                 │
     │ │   └── ollama/Dockerfile        # pulls llama3.x for local fallback    │
     │ (optional profile)                                                      │
     │ │                                                                       │
     │ ├── evals/                       # >= 20 cases; failure taxonomy with   │
     │ per-cat metrics                                                         │
     │ │   ├── cases/                   # YAML; seed_state + expected_outcomes │
     │ │   │   ├── 01_quiet_engineer.yaml                                      │
     │ │   │   ├── 02_scope_creep_diff_too_large.yaml                          │
     │ │   │   ├── 03_shared_blocker_two_engineers.yaml                        │
     │ │   │   ├── 04_offsprint_work.yaml                                      │
     │ │   │   ├── 05_carry_forward_3_days_hot.yaml                            │
     │ │   │   ├── 06_escalation_5_days_hot.yaml                               │
     │ │   │   ├── 07_standup_skipped_one_day.yaml                             │
     │ │   │   ├── 08_estimate_creep.yaml                                      │
     │ │   │   ├── 09_dependency_hotspot_blocks_3.yaml                         │
     │ │   │   ├── 10_midsprint_addition_who_added.yaml                        │
     │ │   │   ├── 11_team_wide_vagueness.yaml                                 │
     │ │   │   ├── 12_resolved_yesterday_flag.yaml                             │
     │ │   │   ├── 13_false_positive_engineer_on_pto.yaml                      │
     │ │   │   ├── 14_premature_stop_should_dig_more.yaml                      │
     │ │   │   ├── 15_tool_arg_hallucination.yaml                              │
     │ │   │   ├── 16_repeated_tool_call_loop.yaml                             │
     │ │   │   ├── 17_self_imposed_blocker.yaml                                │
     │ │   │   ├── 18_correct_NOTE_mode.yaml                                   │
     │ │   │   ├── 19_correct_DM_draft.yaml                                    │
     │ │   │   ├── 20_idempotency_double_send.yaml                             │
     │ │   │   └── 21_readback_failure.yaml                                    │
     │ │   ├── failure_taxonomy.py      # Enum: TOOL_ARG_HALLUCINATION,        │
     │ PREMATURE_STOP,                                                         │
     │ │   │                            #       INFINITE_LOOP,                 │
     │ WRONG_TOOL_SELECTION,                                                   │
     │ │   │                            #       MISSED_HOTSPOT,                │
     │ FALSE_POSITIVE,                                                         │
     │ │   │                            #       RESPONSE_MODE_MISMATCH,        │
     │ IDEMPOTENCY_VIOLATION,                                                  │
     │ │   │                            #       READBACK_FAILURE,              │
     │ ESCALATION_MISS                                                         │
     │ │   ├── runner.py                # parallel; per-case + per-category    │
     │ metrics                                                                 │
     │ │   ├── scoring.py                                                      │
     │ │   ├── replay.py                # load saved trace; re-run LLM only    │
     │ with new prompt                                                         │
     │ │   └── reports/                 # markdown report per run;             │
     │ git-friendly diffs                                                      │
     │ │                                                                       │
     │ └── tests/                                                              │
     │     ├── unit/                    # pydantic models, tool input          │
     │ validation, stop conditions                                             │
     │     ├── integration/             # against compose: end-to-end Phase    │
     │ 0–7 (Phase 8 mocked)                                                    │
     │     └── golden/                  # snapshot tests for brief composition │
     │                                                                         │
     │ ---                                                                     │
     │ Key design decisions — what makes this senior                           │
     │                                                                         │
     │ 1. Tool registry, not 40 copy-pasted files                              │
     │                                                                         │
     │ BaseTool[InputT, OutputT] with:                                         │
     │ - name: str, description: str                                           │
     │ - input_model: type[BaseModel], output_model: type[BaseModel]           │
     │ - idempotency_key(input) -> str (writers only; readers skip)            │
     │ - retry_policy: RetryPolicy (max attempts, backoff, retry-on)           │
     │ - requires_approval: bool (writers default True; Phase 5 ReACT cannot   │
     │ call them)                                                              │
     │ - Returns ToolResult[OutputT] | ToolError — never bare exceptions       │
     │ across the boundary                                                     │
     │                                                                         │
     │ registry.py validates schemas at import time, generates the Anthropic   │
     │ tool                                                                    │
     │ schema list from the Pydantic models, and prevents Phase 5 from binding │
     │  any                                                                    │
     │ requires_approval=True tool.                                            │
     │                                                                         │
     │ 2. ReACT loop with stop conditions and a verifier                       │
     │                                                                         │
     │ AgentLoop.run(hotspot) — explicit step/stop, NOT while True.            │
     │ - StopConditions: max_steps=12, wall_clock=60s, token_budget=30k,       │
     │ no-progress (same tool+args twice in a row → halt + ask), explicit done │
     │  tool                                                                   │
     │ - Verifier: separate LLM call (Sonnet) re-reads the produced answer +   │
     │ the                                                                     │
     │ tool-call transcript. Returns PASS | RETRY_WITH_HINT(reason) | FAIL.    │
     │ - Success-claim detector: if the loop's last LLM message claims         │
     │ completion                                                              │
     │ but no tool calls in the last 2 steps could have produced that claim,   │
     │ force                                                                   │
     │ the verifier to run. This is the "agent confidently lied to me" guard.  │
     │                                                                         │
     │ 3. Concurrency, not serial                                              │
     │                                                                         │
     │ - Phase 1: asyncio.gather across Jira/GitLab/Chat                       │
     │ - Phase 2: per-engineer triage LLM calls in parallel (semaphore on      │
     │ provider RPM)                                                           │
     │ - Phase 5: multiple hotspots run their own ReACT loops concurrently     │
     │ (serial                                                                 │
     │ within one — it's reasoning — parallel across)                          │
     │                                                                         │
     │ 4. Memory tiers — distinct, named, sized                                │
     │                                                                         │
     │ - WorkingContext (in-process, capped at N tokens, compaction =          │
     │ summarize                                                               │
     │ oldest turns into a single "context-so-far" message)                    │
     │ - DurableState (SQLite, the 6 tables from spec + FTS5 on observations)  │
     │ - References (just IDs/paths — fetched on demand, never stored          │
     │ expanded)                                                               │
     │                                                                         │
     │ 5. Model router — proves the abstraction                                │
     │                                                                         │
     │ - Anthropic provider for Opus/Sonnet/Haiku tiers                        │
     │ - Ollama provider for local llama (offline evals, prompt iteration)     │
     │ - config/router.yaml maps (phase, tier) → provider:model; swappable per │
     │  run                                                                    │
     │ - Commits will show: initial Anthropic-only, then Ollama added — both   │
     │ providers                                                               │
     │ shipped under the same interface                                        │
     │                                                                         │
     │ 6. Eval harness with failure taxonomy                                   │
     │                                                                         │
     │ - 20+ YAML cases, each with seed_state (jira_mock fixtures, mattermost  │
     │ postgres snapshot, sqlite preload) and expected_outcomes (which         │
     │ hotspots, which                                                         │
     │ response modes, days-hot counters, escalations)                         │
     │ - FailureTaxonomy enum + per-category pass rate                         │
     │ - make eval-quick (5 cases, < 60s) gates every commit; make eval-full   │
     │ nightly                                                                 │
     │ - evals/replay.py: load a saved trace, swap a prompt version, re-run    │
     │ only the                                                                │
     │ LLM calls — answers "would prompt v2 have caught this regression?"      │
     │                                                                         │
     │ 7. Traces I can reconstruct a bad run from                              │
     │                                                                         │
     │ - OpenTelemetry: phase span → tool spans + LLM spans; each LLM span has │
     │ prompt, response, model, in/out tokens, latency, cache_hit, cost        │
     │ - Phoenix in compose for the span-tree UI                               │
     │ - ALSO dumped to traces/YYYY-MM-DD/<run_id>.jsonl — git-diffable,       │
     │ grep-able                                                               │
     │                                                                         │
     │ 8. Idempotency + readback on every writer                               │
     │                                                                         │
     │ - idempotency.py: hash of (tool_name, normalized_args, run_date) →      │
     │ cached                                                                  │
     │ result; "what if the LLM retries this?" answered by the cache           │
     │ - readback.py: after chat.post_dm, fetch the channel and assert message │
     │  ID                                                                     │
     │   - body match; after jira.post_comment, GET the comment back. Fail     │
     │ loudly                                                                  │
     │ if not. Readback runs against whichever ChatProvider is configured —    │
     │ Mattermost in tests, Slack in production if wired.                      │
     │                                                                         │
     │ 9. Sandboxing posture (honest)                                          │
     │                                                                         │
     │ - The agent doesn't run arbitrary code — every action is a typed        │
     │ Pydantic tool                                                           │
     │ - FTS5 queries are parameterized                                        │
     │ - Writer tools gated behind requires_approval flag; Phase 5 binding     │
     │ excludes                                                                │
     │ them at registration time — defense in depth, not just at the call site │
     │ - README documents this explicitly: "we chose not to add subprocess     │
     │ sandboxing                                                              │
     │ because no tool executes user code; if you add one, here's where"       │
     │                                                                         │
     │ 10. Prompts with provenance                                             │
     │                                                                         │
     │ - prompts/<phase>/vN.md frontmatter: model, temperature, created,       │
     │ author, parent_version, change_reason                                   │
     │ - README.md per phase: the evolution log ("v2: tightened JSON schema    │
     │ after                                                                   │
     │ case 15 hallucinated a ticket key")                                     │
     │ - Loader picks version via config/prompts.yaml — supports A/B during    │
     │ evals                                                                   │
     │                                                                         │
     │ 11. .claude/ directory — build for self                                 │
     │                                                                         │
     │ - commands/eval.md, commands/trace.md, commands/replay.md,              │
     │ commands/seed.md                                                        │
     │ - agents/eval-curator.md (reviews new eval cases for taxonomy coverage) │
     │ - agents/prompt-tuner.md (diffs prompt versions, runs A/B on quick set) │
     │ - hooks/pre_commit.sh → make check                                      │
     │ - MCP server (mcp/server.py) exposes run_evals, get_trace,              │
     │ list_hotspots, replay so Claude Code drives our own iteration loop      │
     │                                                                         │
     │ 12. Audit                                                               │
     │                                                                         │
     │ - decisions table records every Phase 8 approval: timestamp,            │
     │ hotspot_id,                                                             │
     │ proposed_mode, TL_action (approve/reject/edit), final_artifact,         │
     │ trace_id (links back to Phoenix + JSONL)                                │
     │ - /decisions route in web UI = the audit log                            │
     │                                                                         │
     │ 13. README of TRADEOFFS, not features                                   │
     │                                                                         │
     │ Documents what we DIDN'T build and why:                                 │
     │ - "No LangChain — we wanted the loop to be readable in agent/loop.py"   │
     │ - "No vector store — FTS5 is enough for 7 days of standups × 4          │
     │ engineers"                                                              │
     │ - "No multi-tenancy — single-team scope"                                │
     │ - "Mock Jira (not real) — boot time and eval reset; here's the seam to  │
     │ swap"                                                                   │
     │ - "Mattermost is primary, Slack is a stub adapter behind the same       │
     │ ChatProvider interface — Slack code path is real but not gated by tests │
     │ because we don't seed a live workspace; here's the half-day swap        │
     │ recipe"                                                                 │
     │ - "No real OAuth flow against Slack — but Mattermost exercises the full │
     │ refresh/expiry/replay path so the abstraction has teeth"                │
     │                                                                         │
     │ 14. Chat provider — agnostic interface, Mattermost primary, Slack stub  │
     │                                                                         │
     │ - ChatProvider ABC in tools/chat/base.py — six methods cover everything │
     │ the agent needs (post_dm, post_channel, get_messages,                   │
     │ verify_webhook_signature, refresh_oauth_token, health_check)            │
     │ - MattermostProvider is the primary, real implementation — exercises    │
     │ real                                                                    │
     │ REST, real outgoing webhooks with signature verification, real OAuth    │
     │ refresh                                                                 │
     │ against the in-compose Mattermost server. This is where the "OAuth that │
     │ actually refreshes / webhook idempotency / signature verify" senior     │
     │ signals                                                                 │
     │ come from                                                               │
     │ - SlackProvider is a stub — same six methods, real Slack SDK calls, but │
     │  not                                                                    │
     │ gated by integration tests (we don't seed a workspace). README          │
     │ documents the                                                           │
     │ ~half-day delta to flip primary                                         │
     │ - factory.py selects via config/chat.yaml: provider: mattermost         │
     │ - Why this shape: ticks the abstraction-thoughtfulness signal AND the   │
     │ real-API-in-the-loop signal AND gives evals a fully reset-able chat     │
     │ layer                                                                   │
     │ via infra/mattermost/snapshot.sh (pg_dump restore between cases)        │
     │                                                                         │
     │ ---                                                                     │
     │ What the Makefile does                                                  │
     │                                                                         │
     │ make install     # uv sync                                              │
     │ make up          # docker-compose up -d; wait healthchecks; seed        │
     │ make seed        # seed gitlab + jira_mock + mattermost                 │
     │ (users/channels/standups) + sqlite baselines                            │
     │ make snapshot    # pg_dump mattermost-postgres →                        │
     │ fixtures/mattermost.dump (post-seed baseline)                           │
     │ make restore     # restore mattermost-postgres from snapshot —          │
     │ per-eval-case reset                                                     │
     │ make down        # compose down -v                                      │
     │ make check       # ruff format --check, ruff check, mypy --strict,      │
     │ pyright, pytest -m "not integration"                                    │
     │ make test        # pytest (incl. integration; requires `make up`)       │
     │ make eval-quick  # 5 cases, < 60s                                       │
     │ make eval-full   # all 20+ cases                                        │
     │ make eval-report # render markdown report                               │
     │ make run         # tl-agent run --date $(shell date +%F)                │
     │ make web         # uvicorn tl_agent.web.app:app --reload                │
     │ make trace       # open Phoenix UI in browser                           │
     │                                                                         │
     │ ---                                                                     │
     │ Critical files (touched first, in this order)                           │
     │                                                                         │
     │ 1. pyproject.toml, Makefile, .python-version, CLAUDE.md, .gitignore     │
     │ 2. infra/docker-compose.yml (incl. mattermost + mattermost-postgres) +  │
     │ services/jira_mock/ + services/mattermost_seed/ +                       │
     │ infra/mattermost/{config.json,snapshot.sh}                              │
     │ 3. src/tl_agent/models/ (all Pydantic — the contract layer first)       │
     │ 4. src/tl_agent/storage/schema.sql + storage/db.py + storage/repos/*    │
     │ 5. src/tl_agent/tools/base.py + tools/registry.py +                     │
     │ tools/idempotency.py +                                                  │
     │ tools/readback.py +                                                     │
     │ tools/chat/{base.py,mattermost.py,slack.py,factory.py,tools.py}         │
     │ 6. src/tl_agent/llm/router.py + llm/anthropic_provider.py +             │
     │ llm/ollama_provider.py                                                  │
     │ 7. src/tl_agent/obs/tracing.py + obs/spans.py (instrumentation before   │
     │ logic)                                                                  │
     │ 8. src/tl_agent/agent/loop.py + agent/stop_conditions.py +              │
     │ agent/verifier.py                                                       │
     │ 9. src/tl_agent/phases/* + phases/orchestrator.py (in order P0→P8)      │
     │ 10. prompts/<phase>/v1.md (each phase's prompt with provenance          │
     │ frontmatter)                                                            │
     │ 11. src/tl_agent/web/app.py + web/routes/review.py + templates          │
     │ 12. evals/cases/*.yaml + evals/runner.py + evals/failure_taxonomy.py    │
     │ 13. .claude/settings.json + .claude/commands/* + .claude/agents/* +     │
     │ mcp/server.py                                                           │
     │ 14. README.md (last — tradeoffs are clearest after building)            │
     │                                                                         │
     │ Reuse note: nothing exists yet — no prior utilities to reuse. Within    │
     │ the build,                                                              │
     │ the registry/router/tracing modules are the central reuse points;       │
     │ everything                                                              │
     │ else composes them.                                                     │
     │                                                                         │
     │ ---                                                                     │
     │ Verification — how I'll prove it works end-to-end                       │
     │                                                                         │
     │ 1. make up && make seed && make snapshot — compose comes up clean       │
     │ (gitlab, jira_mock, mattermost + its postgres, phoenix all              │
     │ healthchecked);                                                         │
     │ seeds load deterministically; snapshot captures the post-seed baseline  │
     │ so                                                                      │
     │ evals can restore between cases.                                        │
     │ 2. make check — ruff + mypy strict + pyright + unit tests all green.    │
     │ 3. make eval-quick — 5 hand-curated scenarios pass: quiet engineer      │
     │ flagged                                                                 │
     │ YELLOW, scope-creep flagged with diff evidence, shared blocker          │
     │ correlated,                                                             │
     │ carry-forward counter increments, false-positive PTO case stays GREEN.  │
     │ 4. make eval-full — 20+ cases; per-failure-category metrics rendered.   │
     │ Acceptance: ≥ 85% overall, no single category < 60%.                    │
     │ 5. make run for 2026-05-22 — produces a brief at                        │
     │ http://localhost:8080/brief;                                            │
     │ trace visible in Phoenix at http://localhost:6006; JSONL trace written  │
     │ to                                                                      │
     │ traces/2026-05-22/<run_id>.jsonl and grep-reconstructable.              │
     │ 6. Phase 8 approve flow — click approve on a DM hotspot →               │
     │ MattermostProvider                                                      │
     │ posts the DM via real REST → readback fetches it via real REST →        │
     │ decisions                                                               │
     │ row written → audit log shows it. Outgoing-webhook target in            │
     │ services/mattermost_seed/webhook_target.py confirms signature           │
     │ verification                                                            │
     │ and idempotent receipt.                                                 │
     │ 7. Idempotency — re-run the same approval; second call short-circuits   │
     │ via                                                                     │
     │ idempotency cache; Mattermost receives only one message (verified via   │
     │ conversations history fetch).                                           │
     │ 7a. OAuth refresh — integration test forces a token expiry, asserts the │
     │  ChatProvider.refresh_oauth_token path fires and the retry succeeds —   │
     │  exercises the senior signal that the abstraction can actually refresh. │
     │ 7b. Chat-provider swap — flip config/chat.yaml: provider: slack; unit   │
     │  suite still passes (interface conformance test runs against both       │
     │ adapters);                                                              │
     │  integration suite skips with a clear message ("requires                │
     │ SLACK_BOT_TOKEN").                                                      │
     │ 8. Failure injection — kill jira_mock mid-Phase-1; orchestrator         │
     │ surfaces a                                                              │
     │ structured ToolError, the run aborts cleanly with a trace pointing at   │
     │ the                                                                     │
     │ failed span (not a silent partial brief).                               │
     │ 9. Replay — python -m evals.replay 14_premature_stop                    │
     │ --prompt-version=v2                                                     │
     │ loads the saved trace, re-runs Phase 5's LLM with v2, shows the diff in │
     │ verdict — proves the prompt-tuning loop is real.                        │
     │ 10. The conversation test — CLAUDE.md answers "tell me about a time the │
     │ agent confidently lied" in one paragraph + one file pointer             │
     │ (agent/verifier.py + the eval case that catches it).                    │

     Tech-Lead Agentic Workflow — Implementation Plan

     Context

     Greenfield project (empty dir, no git). Build a production-grade tech-lead
     agent
     that runs an 8-phase morning loop over a team of four (John, Matt, Alicia,
     Karen): collect Jira/GitLab/standup signals, triage per-engineer,
     cross-correlate,
     deep-dive uncertain hot spots with a ReACT loop, choose a response mode,
     draft
     artifacts, and require TL approval before any side-effect lands.

     The "why" is the requirements list at the bottom of the user message: this
     is a
     hiring-bar artifact. Every choice should be defensible against the "what to
      look
     for in a senior agent build" checklist — eval harness, traces, idempotency,
      tool
     registry with validation, model router, prompt provenance, .claude/
     hygiene,
     not-built-things, README of tradeoffs.

     Stack (locked in via questions): Python 3.12 + uv; Pydantic v2 everywhere;
     mypy --strict + pyright + ruff; Makefile gates; hybrid Jira-mock (FastAPI)
     +
     real GitLab CE in compose; Anthropic + Ollama model router; OpenTelemetry +
     Phoenix; FastAPI + HTMX review UI; chat-provider-agnostic interface with
     real Mattermost (in compose) as the primary standup source + DM sink, and a
     Slack adapter stub behind the same interface (not gated by tests since we
     don't seed a real workspace — documents the seam).

     ---

     ---
     Key design decisions — what makes this senior

     1. Tool registry, not 40 copy-pasted files

     BaseTool[InputT, OutputT] with:
     - name: str, description: str
     - input_model: type[BaseModel], output_model: type[BaseModel]
     - idempotency_key(input) -> str (writers only; readers skip)
     - retry_policy: RetryPolicy (max attempts, backoff, retry-on)
     - requires_approval: bool (writers default True; Phase 5 ReACT cannot call
     them)
     - Returns ToolResult[OutputT] | ToolError — never bare exceptions across
     the boundary

     registry.py validates schemas at import time, generates the Anthropic tool
     schema list from the Pydantic models, and prevents Phase 5 from binding any
     requires_approval=True tool.

     2. ReACT loop with stop conditions and a verifier

     AgentLoop.run(hotspot) — explicit step/stop, NOT while True.
     - StopConditions: max_steps=12, wall_clock=60s, token_budget=30k,
     no-progress (same tool+args twice in a row → halt + ask), explicit done
     tool
     - Verifier: separate LLM call (Sonnet) re-reads the produced answer + the
     tool-call transcript. Returns PASS | RETRY_WITH_HINT(reason) | FAIL.
     - Success-claim detector: if the loop's last LLM message claims completion
     but no tool calls in the last 2 steps could have produced that claim, force
     the verifier to run. This is the "agent confidently lied to me" guard.

     3. Concurrency, not serial

     - Phase 1: asyncio.gather across Jira/GitLab/Chat
     - Phase 2: per-engineer triage LLM calls in parallel (semaphore on provider
      RPM)
     - Phase 5: multiple hotspots run their own ReACT loops concurrently (serial
     within one — it's reasoning — parallel across)

     4. Memory tiers — distinct, named, sized

     - WorkingContext (in-process, capped at N tokens, compaction = summarize
     oldest turns into a single "context-so-far" message)
     - DurableState (SQLite, the 6 tables from spec + FTS5 on observations)
     - References (just IDs/paths — fetched on demand, never stored expanded)

     5. Model router — proves the abstraction

     - Anthropic provider for Opus/Sonnet/Haiku tiers
     - Ollama provider for local llama (offline evals, prompt iteration)
     - config/router.yaml maps (phase, tier) → provider:model; swappable per run
     - Commits will show: initial Anthropic-only, then Ollama added — both
     providers
     shipped under the same interface

     6. Eval harness with failure taxonomy

     - 20+ YAML cases, each with seed_state (jira_mock fixtures, mattermost
     postgres snapshot, sqlite preload) and expected_outcomes (which hotspots,
     which
     response modes, days-hot counters, escalations)
     - FailureTaxonomy enum + per-category pass rate
     - make eval-quick (5 cases, < 60s) gates every commit; make eval-full
     nightly
     - evals/replay.py: load a saved trace, swap a prompt version, re-run only
     the
     LLM calls — answers "would prompt v2 have caught this regression?"

     7. Traces I can reconstruct a bad run from

     - OpenTelemetry: phase span → tool spans + LLM spans; each LLM span has
     prompt, response, model, in/out tokens, latency, cache_hit, cost
     - Phoenix in compose for the span-tree UI
     - ALSO dumped to traces/YYYY-MM-DD/<run_id>.jsonl — git-diffable, grep-able

     8. Idempotency + readback on every writer

     - idempotency.py: hash of (tool_name, normalized_args, run_date) → cached
     result; "what if the LLM retries this?" answered by the cache
     - readback.py: after chat.post_dm, fetch the channel and assert message ID
       - body match; after jira.post_comment, GET the comment back. Fail loudly
     if not. Readback runs against whichever ChatProvider is configured —
     Mattermost in tests, Slack in production if wired.

     9. Sandboxing posture (honest)

     - The agent doesn't run arbitrary code — every action is a typed Pydantic
     tool
     - FTS5 queries are parameterized
     - Writer tools gated behind requires_approval flag; Phase 5 binding
     excludes
     them at registration time — defense in depth, not just at the call site
     - README documents this explicitly: "we chose not to add subprocess
     sandboxing
     because no tool executes user code; if you add one, here's where"

     10. Prompts with provenance

     - prompts/<phase>/vN.md frontmatter: model, temperature, created,
     author, parent_version, change_reason
     - README.md per phase: the evolution log ("v2: tightened JSON schema after
     case 15 hallucinated a ticket key")
     - Loader picks version via config/prompts.yaml — supports A/B during evals

     11. .claude/ directory — build for self

     - commands/eval.md, commands/trace.md, commands/replay.md, commands/seed.md
     - agents/eval-curator.md (reviews new eval cases for taxonomy coverage)
     - agents/prompt-tuner.md (diffs prompt versions, runs A/B on quick set)
     - hooks/pre_commit.sh → make check
     - MCP server (mcp/server.py) exposes run_evals, get_trace,
     list_hotspots, replay so Claude Code drives our own iteration loop

     12. Audit

     - decisions table records every Phase 8 approval: timestamp, hotspot_id,
     proposed_mode, TL_action (approve/reject/edit), final_artifact,
     trace_id (links back to Phoenix + JSONL)
     - /decisions route in web UI = the audit log

     13. README of TRADEOFFS, not features

     Documents what we DIDN'T build and why:
     - "No LangChain — we wanted the loop to be readable in agent/loop.py"
     - "No vector store — FTS5 is enough for 7 days of standups × 4 engineers"
     - "No multi-tenancy — single-team scope"
     - "Mock Jira (not real) — boot time and eval reset; here's the seam to
     swap"
     - "Mattermost is primary, Slack is a stub adapter behind the same
     ChatProvider interface — Slack code path is real but not gated by tests
     because we don't seed a live workspace; here's the half-day swap recipe"
     - "No real OAuth flow against Slack — but Mattermost exercises the full
     refresh/expiry/replay path so the abstraction has teeth"

     14. Chat provider — agnostic interface, Mattermost primary, Slack stub

     - ChatProvider ABC in tools/chat/base.py — six methods cover everything
     the agent needs (post_dm, post_channel, get_messages,
     verify_webhook_signature, refresh_oauth_token, health_check)
     - MattermostProvider is the primary, real implementation — exercises real
     REST, real outgoing webhooks with signature verification, real OAuth
     refresh
     against the in-compose Mattermost server. This is where the "OAuth that
     actually refreshes / webhook idempotency / signature verify" senior signals
     come from
     - SlackProvider is a stub — same six methods, real Slack SDK calls, but not
     gated by integration tests (we don't seed a workspace). README documents
     the
     ~half-day delta to flip primary
     - factory.py selects via config/chat.yaml: provider: mattermost
     - Why this shape: ticks the abstraction-thoughtfulness signal AND the
     real-API-in-the-loop signal AND gives evals a fully reset-able chat layer
     via infra/mattermost/snapshot.sh (pg_dump restore between cases)

     ---
     What the Makefile does

     make install     # uv sync
     make up          # docker-compose up -d; wait healthchecks; seed
     make seed        # seed gitlab + jira_mock + mattermost
     (users/channels/standups) + sqlite baselines
     make snapshot    # pg_dump mattermost-postgres → fixtures/mattermost.dump
     (post-seed baseline)
     make restore     # restore mattermost-postgres from snapshot —
     per-eval-case reset
     make down        # compose down -v
     make check       # ruff format --check, ruff check, mypy --strict, pyright,
      pytest -m "not integration"
     make test        # pytest (incl. integration; requires `make up`)
     make eval-quick  # 5 cases, < 60s
     make eval-full   # all 20+ cases
     make eval-report # render markdown report
     make run         # tl-agent run --date $(shell date +%F)
     make web         # uvicorn tl_agent.web.app:app --reload
     make trace       # open Phoenix UI in browser

     ---
     Critical files (touched first, in this order)

     1. pyproject.toml, Makefile, .python-version, CLAUDE.md, .gitignore
     2. infra/docker-compose.yml (incl. mattermost + mattermost-postgres) +
     services/jira_mock/ + services/mattermost_seed/ +
     infra/mattermost/{config.json,snapshot.sh}
     3. src/tl_agent/models/ (all Pydantic — the contract layer first)
     4. src/tl_agent/storage/schema.sql + storage/db.py + storage/repos/*
     5. src/tl_agent/tools/base.py + tools/registry.py + tools/idempotency.py +
     tools/readback.py +
     tools/chat/{base.py,mattermost.py,slack.py,factory.py,tools.py}
     6. src/tl_agent/llm/router.py + llm/anthropic_provider.py +
     llm/ollama_provider.py
     7. src/tl_agent/obs/tracing.py + obs/spans.py (instrumentation before
     logic)
     8. src/tl_agent/agent/loop.py + agent/stop_conditions.py +
     agent/verifier.py
     9. src/tl_agent/phases/* + phases/orchestrator.py (in order P0→P8)
     10. prompts/<phase>/v1.md (each phase's prompt with provenance frontmatter)
     11. src/tl_agent/web/app.py + web/routes/review.py + templates
     12. evals/cases/*.yaml + evals/runner.py + evals/failure_taxonomy.py
     13. .claude/settings.json + .claude/commands/* + .claude/agents/* +
     mcp/server.py
     14. README.md (last — tradeoffs are clearest after building)

     Reuse note: nothing exists yet — no prior utilities to reuse. Within the
     build,
     the registry/router/tracing modules are the central reuse points;
     everything
     else composes them.

     ---
     Verification — how I'll prove it works end-to-end

     1. make up && make seed && make snapshot — compose comes up clean
     (gitlab, jira_mock, mattermost + its postgres, phoenix all healthchecked);
     seeds load deterministically; snapshot captures the post-seed baseline so
     evals can restore between cases.
     2. make check — ruff + mypy strict + pyright + unit tests all green.
     3. make eval-quick — 5 hand-curated scenarios pass: quiet engineer flagged
     YELLOW, scope-creep flagged with diff evidence, shared blocker correlated,
     carry-forward counter increments, false-positive PTO case stays GREEN.
     4. make eval-full — 20+ cases; per-failure-category metrics rendered.
     Acceptance: ≥ 85% overall, no single category < 60%.
     5. make run for 2026-05-22 — produces a brief at
     http://localhost:8080/brief;
     trace visible in Phoenix at http://localhost:6006; JSONL trace written to
     traces/2026-05-22/<run_id>.jsonl and grep-reconstructable.
     6. Phase 8 approve flow — click approve on a DM hotspot →
     MattermostProvider
     posts the DM via real REST → readback fetches it via real REST → decisions
     row written → audit log shows it. Outgoing-webhook target in
     services/mattermost_seed/webhook_target.py confirms signature verification
     and idempotent receipt.
     7. Idempotency — re-run the same approval; second call short-circuits via
     idempotency cache; Mattermost receives only one message (verified via
     conversations history fetch).
     7a. OAuth refresh — integration test forces a token expiry, asserts the
      ChatProvider.refresh_oauth_token path fires and the retry succeeds —
      exercises the senior signal that the abstraction can actually refresh.
     7b. Chat-provider swap — flip config/chat.yaml: provider: slack; unit
      suite still passes (interface conformance test runs against both
     adapters);
      integration suite skips with a clear message ("requires SLACK_BOT_TOKEN").
     8. Failure injection — kill jira_mock mid-Phase-1; orchestrator surfaces a
     structured ToolError, the run aborts cleanly with a trace pointing at the
     failed span (not a silent partial brief).
     9. Replay — python -m evals.replay 14_premature_stop --prompt-version=v2
     loads the saved trace, re-runs Phase 5's LLM with v2, shows the diff in
     verdict — proves the prompt-tuning loop is real.
     10. The conversation test — CLAUDE.md answers "tell me about a time the
     agent confidently lied" in one paragraph + one file pointer
     (agent/verifier.py + the eval case that catches it).

⏺ Pausing to update you on progress and check direction.

  Status: 7/16 tasks complete, 63 unit tests green, 8 commits. The "core" layer
  is built — models, storage (markdown + SQLite + FTS5), tool registry with
  idempotency/readback, LLM router (Anthropic + Ollama behind one ABC),
  OpenTelemetry observability with JSONL sink, and the ReACT loop with stop
  conditions + the "agent confidently lied" verifier (both deterministic
  claim-checker and LLM-driven).

  What's left, ranked by leverage:
  1. Concrete tools (Jira, GitLab, chat) — needed for any end-to-end run
  2. 8 phases + orchestrator — wires everything together; first runnable thing
  3. Compose stack (real GitLab + Mattermost + jira_mock + Phoenix) — needed for
   integration tests
  4. Prompts with provenance — small but content-heavy
  5. Eval harness with 20+ cases — proves the senior signal
  6. Web UI for Phase 8 review
  7. .claude/ hygiene + MCP server
  8. Final README/CLAUDE pass for tradeoffs

  Realistic for what remains in this session: I can land #1, #2, #3, and a
  stubbed #4 to get end-to-end "make up && make seed && make run" actually

       34  ## Tradeoffs — what we did NOT build, and why
       35  
       36  ### No LangChain / CrewAI / AutoGen wrapper
       19 -The control loop is in `src/tl_agent/agent/loop.py` — about 20
          -0 lines.                                                      
       20 -Wrapper frameworks hide the layer that matters when an agent m
          -isbehaves                                                     
       21 -(stop conditions, verifier feedback, tool-arg validation), and
          - "we built it on                                              
       22 -LangChain" is not a credible answer to "tell me about a time t
          -he agent                                                      
       23 -confidently lied to you." We accept the cost of writing our ow
          -n scaffolding so                                              
       24 -the loop is debuggable.                                       
       37  
       38 +The control loop is `src/tl_agent/agent/loop.py` — about 250 l
          +ines, explicit                                                
       39 +`step()` / `stop()`, stop reasons as a named enum. Wrapper fra
          +meworks hide                                                  
       40 +the layer that matters when an agent misbehaves (stop conditio
          +ns, verifier                                                  
       41 +feedback, tool-arg validation). "We built it on LangChain" is 
          +not a credible                                                
       42 +answer to "tell me about a time the agent confidently lied." W
          +e accept the                                                  
       43 +cost of writing our own scaffolding so the loop is debuggable.
       44 +                                                              
       45  ### No vector store

       47  The corpus is 4 engineers × 7 days of standups + a sprint's wo
           rth of tickets.
       28 -SQLite FTS5 covers `search_standup_history` in <1 ms per query
          - with zero extra                                              
       29 -infra. If the team grows past ~20 engineers or the standup win
          -dow past a                                                    
       30 -quarter, swap in pgvector behind the `memory.search_standup_hi
          -story` tool —                                                 
       31 -the interface won't change.                                   
       48 +SQLite FTS5 covers `search_standup_history` in sub-millisecond
          + per query with                                               
       49 +zero extra infra. If the team grows past ~20 engineers or the 
          +standup window

          +chStandupHistoryTool`                                         
       51 +— the interface won't change.                                 
       52  
       53  ### No multi-tenancy
       54 +

           e team. Adding

          -ilter in every

      104 +                                                              
      105 +### Idempotency via SQLite, not Redis                         
      106 +                                                              
      107 +The dedup load is one row per writer call per day. SQLite cove
          +rs it; adding                                                 
      108 +Redis would mean an extra service to run, more network hops, a
          +nd a new                                                      
      109 +failure mode. If you outgrow it, the swap is behind `Idempoten
          +cyLookup` in

       57  ## How to work here
       58  
       34 -- `make check` is the gate. Run before every commit. It runs r
          -uff (format + lint), mypy strict, pyright strict, and unit tes
          -ts.                                                           
       35 -- `make eval-quick` runs 5 scenarios in <60s. Run before mergi
          -ng anything touching phases, prompts, or tools.               
       36 -- All new tools subclass `BaseTool`. Define Pydantic input/out
          -put models, an `idempotency_key` (writers only), and `requires
          -_approval`. The registry validates at import time — schema err
          -ors fail loudly.                                              
       37 -- All new prompts get a new version file (`vN+1.md`) with fron
          -tmatter recording `parent_version` and `change_reason`. Never 
          -edit a vN.md in place — that breaks reproducibility of past ru
          -ns.                                                           
       38 -- All new phases get an OTel span via `@phase_span`. All new t
          -ools get `@tool_span`. All LLM calls go through the router (wh
          -ich emits `@llm_span`).                                       
       39 -- Writer tools (`requires_approval=True`) cannot be bound to P
          -hase 5's ReACT loop. The registry enforces this — do not bypas
          -s it.                                                         
       59 +- **`make check` is the gate.** Run before every commit. Hooks
          + in                                                           
       60 +  `.claude/hooks/pre_commit.sh` enforce this.                 
       61 +- **`make eval-quick`** runs 5 scenarios in <60s. Run before m
          +erging                                                        
       62 +  anything touching phases, prompts, or tools.                
       63 +- **New tools subclass `BaseTool`.** Define Pydantic input/out
          +put models,                                                   
       64 +  declare `idempotency_key` (writers only), set `requires_appr
          +oval`. The                                                    
       65 +  registry validates at import time — schema errors fail loudl
          +y.                                                            
       66 +- **New prompts get a new version file** (`vN+1.md`) with fron
          +tmatter                                                       
       67 +  recording `parent_version` and `change_reason`. Never edit a
          + vN.md in                                                     
       68 +  place — that breaks reproducibility of past runs.           
       69 +- **New phases get an OTel span via `@phase_span`.** New tools
          + get                                                          
       70 +  `@tool_span` (via `BaseTool.invoke`). All LLM calls go throu
          +gh the                                                        
       71 +  router which emits `llm_span`.                              
       72 +- **Writer tools (`requires_approval=True`) cannot be bound to
          + Phase 5's                                                    
       73 +  ReACT loop.** The registry enforces this — do not bypass it.

       88  ## Conventions
       89  
       90  - Imports: stdlib → third-party → local; sorted by ruff.
       91  - Strings: double quotes; f-strings preferred over `.format`.
       92  - Async: prefer `anyio` over raw asyncio when both work; `asyn
           cio` is fine when needed.
       56 -- Logging: structured via `structlog` if added; until then, `l
          -ogger.info("event", k=v)` style.                              
       57 -- Comments: rarely. Identifier names should carry the WHAT; co
          -mments are for the WHY when non-obvious.                      
       93 +- Logging: structured via `logger.info("event", extra={"k": v}
          +)`.                                                           
       94 +- Comments: rare. Identifier names carry the WHAT; comments ex
          +plain WHY when non-obvious.                                   
       95  
       96  ## Things to avoid
       97  
       98  - LangChain / CrewAI / AutoGen — see README.md for the rationa
           le.
       99  - Bypassing the registry to call a tool directly — defeats val
           idation, retry, idempotency, audit.
      100  - Editing prompt files in place — always bump the version.
       64 -- Hardcoding a model name outside `config/router.yaml` — use `
          -router.for_phase(phase, tier)`.                               
      101 +- Hardcoding a model name outside `config/router.yaml` — use `
          +router.for_phase(phase)`.                                     
      102  - Mocking the database in tests — use the real SQLite, in tmp 
           dir. Mock the network instead.
      103 +- Adding deps without thought — `pyproject.toml` already cover
          +s the essentials; new deps need a sentence of justification in
          + the commit.
